Document 035: 20261104【投研報告】市場快訊_美期中選舉牽動市場表現，股債配置重品質、控風險

美期中選舉牽動市場表現，股債配置重品質、控風險
2026 年 11 月 04 日
一、 市場動態
1. 美期中選舉牽動市場情緒，亞股一度隨美股期貨下挫：美國 11 月 3 日舉行期中選舉
，根據 CNN 網站的結果顯示，截至台北時間 11/4 下午 3 點左右，民主黨於參議院
已取得 100 席中的 53 席的過半席次，於眾議院則在 435 席中取得了 225 席(218 席
過半)。市場重新評估國會控制權及川普政府未來政策推動能力，擔心民主黨國會影響
力擴大，可能對財政政策及科技企業監管形成新的制衡。S&P 500 期貨與 Nasdaq 100

期貨在開票期間一度下挫 1.38%及 2.35%，科技股明顯承受較大賣壓。惟隨後跌深反
彈，截至台北時間下午 3 時左右，S&P 500 期貨與 Nasdaq 100 期貨跌幅已收斂至
0.22%及 0.44%。亞洲市場方面，科技股為主及出口導向股市受到美股期貨拖累，台
灣加權指數 11/4 盤中最低下挫 3.38%，所幸隨跌幅收斂，終場下跌 0.83%；韓股同
日也由大跌近 4%由黑翻紅上漲 0.62%。11/3 未開盤的日股，在 11/4 開盤後出現補
跌，最低下挫近 4.8%，但最後跌幅收斂至下跌 1.42%。
2. 美國公債及美元同步反映政策預期調整，市場避險情緒升溫：美國公債及美元反映政
策預期調整，殖利率盤中先跌後漲：10 年期公債殖利率盤中先跌後漲，截至台北時間
下午 3 時來到 4.906%；同一時間，美元指數小跌約 0.37%。股、債、匯市的變動顯
示，投資人除了調整股票風險部位，也在評估未來美國財政刺激規模、政府債務供給
與利率前景。
二、 下跌原因
1. 民主黨取得國會控制權，市場重新定價財政與監管政策：本次選舉對金融市場的主要

影響，在於未來行政與立法部門的權力分配可能改變。共和黨先前同時掌握白宮與國
會，有利於推動稅制、財政及產業政策；民主黨在參眾兩院均取得過半席次後，部分

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
政策將面臨更高的協商成本。財政方面，新增減稅及擴大支出的空間可能受到限制，
市場因而下修部分財政刺激預期；企業方面，國會監督權可能增加對大型科技企業、
AI 資料中心、反壟斷及資料隱私議題的調查壓力。惟國會控制權改變不代表既有政策
立即逆轉，也不代表所有監管措施均能完成立法。此次市場反應更可能是投資人重新
要求政策風險溢酬，而非企業獲利已發生實質下修。
2. AI 資本支出與投資回報疑慮尚未消退，放大科技股對政治事件的反應：此次選舉開票
期間，Nasdaq 期貨及與科技相關的亞股出現大幅波動，亦與近期科技股基本面及評

價爭議有關。10 月底以來美股因大型科技企業 AI 投資效益受到質疑而大幅修正，市
場關注焦點逐漸由 AI 基礎建設支出規模，轉向相關投資能否創造足夠的營運現金流與
股東報酬。以雲端服務及資料中心產業而言，企業雖可透過 AI 運算需求增加帶動營收
成長，但 GPU、伺服器、電力設備及資料中心建設需要龐大前期資金，若營收回收速
度不如預期，可能使自由現金流承壓並增加外部融資需求。近期部分科技企業籌資計
畫及財測不佳，已使投資人提高對財務品質的要求。當市場原先已對 AI 投資回報存有
疑慮，新的政治及監管不確定性便更容易放大評價修正幅度。
三、 市場看法
1. 半導體需求仍具分化，政治事件不等於 AI 產業成長趨勢反轉：科技股近期的股價修正
與 AI 終端需求惡化並非相同概念。AMD 近期公布優於市場預期的財報，並對資料中
心業務提出較強勁的成長展望，顯示 AI 運算需求仍具有一定韌性；相對而言，Intel
先前財測疲弱及現金流壓力，則反映不同半導體企業的產品競爭力、資本效率及終端
市場曝險存在明顯差異。因此，不宜將半導體股整體下跌解讀為 AI 需求全面降溫。從

產業鏈觀察，具備技術領先優勢、穩定客戶需求及良好現金流的企業，與高度依賴外
部融資、尚未形成穩定獲利模式的基礎建設業者，對資金成本及監管變化的承受能力
並不相同。未來市場可能更加重視個別企業的獲利品質，而非單純以 AI 相關營收成長
作為估值依據。
2. 政治事件可能延長科技股評價調整，但中期走勢仍取決於企業獲利：本次期中選舉引
發的期貨跌勢，反映市場對政策方向及國會控制權變化的即時反應，惟政治不確定性
通常須透過實際立法、企業投資計畫或經濟活動變化，才會形成較持久的基本面影響
。從企業獲利角度觀察，路透社引用 LSEG 資料指出，S&P 500 第三季企業獲利仍呈
現強勁成長，科技企業對整體獲利貢獻顯著，顯示目前尚無充分證據支持美國企業基
本面全面轉弱。不過，科技股先前已累積較高成長預期，市場對資本支出回報及監管
風險的容忍度降低，可能使股價即使在營收成長下仍承受評價壓力。短期而言，若選
舉結果逐漸明朗，且企業獲利預估未進一步下修，市場有機會逐步消化政治風險；反
之，若政策不確定性伴隨獲利預期下修，修正時間可能延長。

策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
3. 歷史經驗顯示選後股市多正向，後續表現仍宜留意基本面變化：根據彭博統計，1950
年以來美國歷次期中選舉後 12 個月，S&P 500 平均報酬率約 15%，且 19 次全數
收正。其中最高為 1954 年的逾 30%，表現最弱的 1986 年亦小幅上漲。我們認為
，選前政策不確定性消除加上府會分治抑制財政與監管的轉向是為歷史上選後行情的
主要支撐，但相關數據所呈現的為過往經驗，未來股市表現能否延續先前情況仍應觀
察後續基本面變化。
4. 美國公債具避險價值，但利率下行能否持續仍取決於通膨與財政前景：在美國期中選

舉開票期間，美股期貨下跌伴隨美債殖利率回落，顯示公債仍能在股市波動期間提供
一定的風險分散效果。然而，本次殖利率下降可能同時包含避險買盤、財政刺激預期
下修及市場對經濟成長前景重新評估等因素，不宜直接解讀為聯準會即將轉向寬鬆。
尤其能源市場仍受到中東地緣政治及供應中斷風險影響，油價波動可能使通膨前景反
覆，限制長天期公債殖利率的下行空間。若未來財政擴張受到限制，可能對長端利率
形成部分支撐；但若政治僵局提高債務上限協商風險，亦可能使美債期限溢酬波動。
因此，債券配置仍須同時考量收益率、存續期間與通膨風險，不宜僅根據單日行情決
定配置方向。
5. 信用市場是判斷風險是否由股權評價擴散至金融條件的重要指標：目前科技股修正主
要反映評價與政策風險，但若 AI 企業持續增加資本支出及外部融資，市場對債務負擔
與償債能力的關注可能進一步升高。對大型投資等級科技企業而言，較穩定的營運現
金流及融資管道有助於吸收資本支出壓力；但對依賴單一客戶合約、設備抵押融資或
高槓桿經營的 AI 基礎建設業者，利率與信用利差上升可能更直接影響投資回報及再融

資能力。後續應追蹤投資等級債與高收益債 OAS、科技及雲端相關發行人利差、新債
發行需求及信用評等展望。若科技股下跌但整體信用利差維持穩定，市場風險可能仍
以股權評價調整為主；若信用利差同步持續擴大，則須提高對金融條件收緊及風險跨
市場擴散的警覺。

不另行通知。
四、 投資建議：
1. 科技股中期成長趨勢仍具支撐，惟短線宜審慎布局並重視個股分化：美國期中選舉帶
來的政策不確定性，短期可能使科技股評價承壓，惟大型雲端業者持續投入 AI 基礎建
設，半導體及相關設備需求仍具中期成長動能，目前尚不宜因美股期貨在開票期間的
下挫即全面轉向保守。我們仍建議維持美股核心配置，但短期避免過度集中於高評價
、高資本支出及高度依賴外部融資的企業。產業方面，資訊科技維持選擇性配置，優
先關注具備技術競爭優勢、穩定營運現金流及較高投資回報能見度的企業；對雲端基
礎建設及 AI 融資相關標的，則應提高財務結構與現金流審查標準。

2. 維持中天期美國公債及高信評投資等級債配置，兼顧收益與風險分散：在美國政策風
險升溫及科技股評價調整期間，美國公債仍具投資組合避險功能，但考量通膨與能源
價格的不確定性，現階段不建議延長存續期間。建議以中天期美國公債及高信評投資
等級債作為主要配置方向，兼顧票息收益、利率敏感度與資產配置穩定性。信用債方
面，建議優先選擇營運現金流充足、槓桿適中及再融資需求可控的發行人，對 AI 相關
企業則須進一步評估資本支出、合約現金流與債務到期結構。

Document 039: 20261116【投研報告】市場快訊_輝達產品時程恐因領導層變動遞延，台股供應鏈重挫

輝達產品時程恐因領導層變動遞延，台股供應鏈重挫
2026 年 11 月 16 日
一、市場動態
1. 輝達執行長閃辭衝擊延燒，台股重挫逾 2500 點：黃仁勳於 11 月 12 日辭去輝達總裁、
執行長及董事職務，由財務長 Colette Kress 暫代執行長，資深董事 Harvey Jones 出
任首任董事長，市場擔憂產品時程與決策速度出現不確定，加上本周財報即將發布，
加深市場觀望情緒。台股今日受上週五美股半導體重挫拖累開低走低，收最低至
41,040 點、下跌 2635 點或 6.03%。台積電跌 135 元或 5.74%，聯發科跌逾 7%，台

達電、創意及台光電、欣興等 PCB／載板族群觸及跌停，高價 AI 股成為賣壓重心。
2. AI 晶片族群同步降溫，資金由「換股」轉為「降低整體曝險」：輝達換帥後，市場原預
期資金將轉進 AMD、博通及 ASIC 等替代方案，惟上週五美國半導體族群全面回檔，
替代受惠股跌幅甚至更大，顯示投資人擔憂的已非單一公司接班問題，而是 AI 投資循
環的整體風險。
二、下跌原因
1. 領導層更替落地，產品時程與決策速度出現不確定：輝達在此事件之前，約 60 名高階
主管直接向黃仁勳匯報，權力高度集中，接班空窗期決策速度恐下降。即使在其任內，
年更節奏已觸及供應鏈物理極限，Rubin CPX 已移出路線圖，Kyber 機櫃傳延至 2028
年。新任管理層若偏重成本與執行穩定，路線圖可能改為「大世代兩年、中間推出強
化版」，800V HVDC、共同封裝光學（CPO）、 M9 等級銅箔基板等升級導入時點恐再
遞延。
2. 規格升級放緩衝擊「本夢比」，高價零組件股首當其衝：台廠 2027 年成長有相當部分
來自 Vera Rubin 單櫃價值約為 GB300 兩倍，而單櫃價值主要來自規格升級。市場擔

憂高階液冷、HVDC 電源與光互連若由 2027 年遞延至 2028 年放量，供應鏈單價、
毛利率與 EPS 成長預估恐面臨下修；缺乏技術突破支撐的高本益比族群評價修正，今
日 PCB／CCL、散熱及電源族群跌停家數最多，即反映此一疑慮。
3. 生態系融資與出口管制成為新增變數：市場估計輝達每季自由現金流約 500 億至 600
億美元，若過渡期暫緩投資及融資交易，生態系資金供給可能暫時減少 500 億美元以
上；據報 Kress 曾反對為 OpenAI 提供最高 1,050 億美元擔保，新增融資審核恐趨嚴，
依賴輝達融資的 NeoCloud 及其供應商受影響較大。此外，事件涉及晶片流向中國之
指控，美方據報擬將訂定較嚴格出口限制，市場估潛在曝險約占輝達營收一成。
4. 期中選舉後，可能的出口管制收緊以及短線獲利了結需求：此事件涉及晶片流向中國之
指控，出口管制收緊將衝擊整體 AI 晶片；台股上週反彈近 4,000 點，短線獲利了結需
求升高，避險心態未退，放大 AI 權值股賣壓。

三、市場看法
1. 2027 年出貨大致鎖定，衝擊先落在評價而非營收：Vera Rubin 已於 8 月量產出貨、
2027 年放量；輝達對供應商之採購與產能承諾達 2,790 億美元，台積電、HBM 及主
要組裝廠 2027 年產能多已預訂。廣達法說表示迄今未見客戶調整訂單，鴻海亦預期
明年 AI 機櫃出貨高雙位數成長。依 ToAlpha 基本情境推算，輝達 CoWoS 份額相對
原本路徑 2027、2028 年僅分別減少 2 與 4 個百分點，CoWoS 總量僅減約 0.6%與

0.8%，影響主要在 2028 年後的產品節奏。
表一：輝達份額變動對 AI 平台與台股供應鏈之影響（情境推估）
項目（相對原本路徑） 2027 年 2028 年
輝達占 CoWoS 需求份額 −2 個百分點 −4 個百分點
輝達平台出貨量 −4.4% −8.9%
AMD 與 ASIC 平台出貨量 +2.5% +5.8%
CoWoS 總量（台積電、封測） −0.6% −0.8%

GPU 與 ASIC 兩邊都有之台股 AI 營收 −1.3% −2.3%

資料來源：Bloomberg，元大銀行整理；此為 Bloomberg 情境推估之 AI 營收影響，非盈餘預估
2. 台股供應鏈由分化轉為齊跌，ASIC 族群抗跌性消失：依曝險分組，10 月 29 日至 11
月 12 日以輝達平台為主者平均下跌 14.0%，GPU 與 ASIC 兩邊都有者下跌 17.0%，以
ASIC 為主者下跌 9.4%，同期加權指數下跌 7.1%。兩邊都有之族群多為前波漲幅大的

規格升級零組件股，跌幅反而最深，顯示市場賣的是「評價」而非「訂單」；今日台股
盤勢延續上周五費半情緒，以 ASIC 為主及兩邊都有之代表股平均重挫逾 8%，以輝達
平台為主者跌約 4.4%，顯示賣壓轉向前波漲多、評價較高的 ASIC 與規格升級股，市
場已由「區分受益者」轉為「全面降低 AI 曝險」，短線錯殺機會浮現。

3. 算力多元化早於本次事件，Google TPU 與 AMD 最直接受惠：根據彭博在本次事件發
生前的統計，市場預估輝達占全球 CoWoS 需求比重將由 2026 年 56%降至 2027 年
45%，AMD 由 9%升至 20%，聯發科由 3%升至 7%，預計本次事件發生後，此趨勢
可能進一步加劇。由於 AMD、博通與雲端業者自研 ASIC 同樣高度依賴台積電先進製
程及台廠組裝與零組件，訂單多在台灣供應鏈內部移轉，台灣在 AMD、Google 供應
鏈同樣扮演重要角色。

4. 歷史經驗顯示 CEO 更替後股價無一致方向，基本面才是中期關鍵：回顧美股大型科技
公司交棒案例，計畫性交棒如 Apple、Microsoft 後 12 個月股價分別上漲 77.1%與
20.1%，但同為規劃性交棒的 Amazon 卻下跌 35.3%；突發撤換的 Intel 兩案，3 個月
內均下跌逾 9%，12 個月後則轉為正報酬。惟過去歷史經驗僅能作為參考，輝達後續
走勢仍取決於事件發展與基本面變化而定。

5. 本週三大事件為短線關鍵驗證點：（一）輝達將於美東 11 月 17 日（台灣時間 11 月 18
日凌晨）公布第三季財報，為首度由暫代執行長主持之法說會，焦點在 2028 會計年度
約七成成長指引是否重申、Vera Rubin 放量節奏及新增融資態度；（二）Anthropic 預
計 11 月 18 日定價上市，為檢驗 AI 市場熱度之指標；（三）黃仁勳預計感恩節前赴國
會作證，牽動晶片出口管制法案走向。若財報與指引穩健，市場有望由情緒面回歸基本

面。
四、投資建議
1. 科技股短期審慎、中期仍正向，AI 供應鏈基本面持續具支撐：黃仁勳辭任使輝達面臨
管理層接班、企業治理及政策調查等不確定性，加上 Fed 升息預期及 AI 資料中心監
管疑慮，市場短線波動可能延續。然而，該事件並未直接改變 AI 算力需求，且上週
CoreWeave Q3 財報營收年增 168%、台積電 10 月營收年增 59.7%，均顯示 AI 基礎
建設投資仍具動能。整體對科技股維持短期審慎、但中長期基本面仍在，台灣及日本
等具技術競爭優勢、獲利與現金流穩健的半導體及 AI 供應鏈亦值得留意。惟市場焦點
已由資本支出規模轉向投資報酬與現金流，後續仍須關注輝達財報及管理團隊能否維
持客戶信心。
2. 以股債平衡穿越波動，善用多元資產降低單一事件風險：台股權重高度集中於台積電
與 AI 供應鏈，對單一事件敏感度高。建議將集中度過高的台股科技部位，適度轉向全
球型或多重資產商品，搭配中天期美國公債及高信評投資等級債穩定收益；待接班人
選、財報指引與出口管制方向明朗後，再逐步提高風險資產比重。

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We review Lotes’s 3Q26 results, released on 13 November.

What’s the impact: Business outlook. Ongoing gross margin dilution due 
to rising raw material costs and less benefits from the AI server trend has 
been, in our view, the primary drag on Lotes’s share price over this year. 
We believe this trend may reverse now as investors are worried about the 
potential AI capex cuts impacting the overall Taiwan supply chain’s revenue 
outlook in 2028, whereas Lotes only has 3-5% of exposure currently. Also, 
management expects gross margin pressure to start easing with the new 
product mass production (eg, SOCAMM) starting from 4Q26. Second, 
Lotes’s regular server-related products may see less chance for inventory 
correction as its products are not in shortage now. Lastly, its desktop 
business has suffered more than the overall industry’s headwinds (ie, unit 
decline 10-15% YoY) as its desktop products had to downgrade from DDR5 
to DDR4/3 due to component shortages, which further negatively impacted 
profitability. We believe these three headwinds will gradually transform into 
tailwinds once the AI capex starts to slow down. However, if the AI server 
capex trend remains as is, we could also see some upside for the 
company. Lotes is still eager to develop a GPU socket for the Rubin Ultra 
generation, which we believe could become a new growth driver. Besides, 
quick disconnect (QD) and NPO sockets might also become growth drivers. 
While QD only accounts for low single digit of total revenue from 3Q26, we 
expect it to have a chance to increase to c.10% of total revenue in 2027E.

3Q26 profitability missed expectations. Lotes’s 3Q26 earnings came in 
at TWD1.92bn (EPS: TWD17.1), which was 11%/14% lower than our/street 
estimates. Despite revenue largely in-line with expectations, gross margin 
decreased by 0.6pp QoQ to 44.8%, mainly from rising copper and gold 
prices; unfavourable product mix (rising high-speed cable and connect and 
QD) may be another reason. However, operating margin rose 0.9pp QoQ to 
22.9% mainly on the lack of one-off employee stock bonus expense (as 
done in 2Q26) but opex was still higher than the company’s guidance 
(<TWD2.0bn) due to salary increase for its Chinese branches.

What we recommend: We lower 2026-27E EPS by 2-7% to factor in 3Q26 
results and lower profitability recovery assumption but maintain 2028E 
EPS. We reaffirm our Buy (1) call but lower our 12M TP to TWD2,480 (from 
TWD2,850), based on a lower PER of 22x (from 28x; high end of its past-5-
year range of 10-28x), on our 1-year forward EPS, given the weaker market 
sentiment. Key risk: lower-than-expected adoption of server CPU platforms.

How we differ: Our 2027-28E revenues are 5-10% higher than the 
Bloomberg consensus, likely due to our more bullish view on QDs.

13 November 2026

3Q26 review: bottomed-out gross margin

➢ 3Q GM slightly declined on rising material cost but likely bottomed out 
➢ Rising QD, SOCAMM, NPO sockets to be key growth drivers in 2027 
➢ Reiterating our Buy (1) rating; lowering 12-month TP to TWD2,480

Company profile 
Founded in 1986 and listed on the Taiwan Stock Exchange in 2007, Lotes is one of the world’s leading 
connector vendors specialising in CPU socket/ILM and connectors, such as DDR, PCIe, SATA, and 
Type-C, with most of its production capacities at its Guangzhou, Zhongshan and Suzhou plants. Its 
superior manufacturing capability and strong client relationships are the key differentiators that make 
Lotes stand out from its competitors. Since the PC market became saturated from 2015, Lotes has 
focused on penetrating non-PC connector businesses, such as connectors for servers, automotive and 
industrial, and new types of cables (eg, Thunderbolt cable).

### Page 5

5

Lotes (3533 TT): 13 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 13 Nov 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
Lotes’ board consists of 7 directors, including 3 independent directors. The total number of
directors is slightly lower than other listed companies in our coverage. Lotes held 8 board
meetings in 2025 and the actual attendance rate was 95%.
Although the Chairman and CEO are not the same person, they are, in fact, brothers and the co-
founders of Lotes. It gives us a sense that Lotes is still a family enterprise, which is likely a
negative for the board and executive management. That said, the background of independent
directors is diversified with expertise in technology and management. We see it as a positive for
the board's quality.
Capital management 2
Lotes declared a DPS of TWD41.5 in 2025, representing a payout ratio of 50%. Being a leading
connector vendor for applications such as desktops, notebooks and servers, Lotes grew rapidly
along with its key clients (Intel and AMD) on strong technical capabilities and higher growth rate
from the server market. The solid revenue CAGR in the past decade required higher working
capital and continuous capex to expand its capacity, which could also be the culprit for its slightly
lower payout ratio compared with other electronic component makers. Having said that, we think
Lotes’ higher revenue growth should partially offset the drawback of a lower payout ratio.
Related party &
transaction 1
In 2025, there were no disclosed related-party sales, while purchases from related parties
amounted to TWD4.4m, representing 0.03% of total cost of sales. We see limited risk from
related-party transactions.
S Supply chain management 2
Lotes established its RBA management system in 2010 and requires its suppliers to meet the
standards of no child labour, no forced labour, treating employees with respect and dignity, and
caring for labourers' safety and health. Lotes hosts a yearly supplier conference to promote the
RBA guidelines to its suppliers. In 2025, 187 suppliers participated in the annual supplier
conference. Lotes also requires its suppliers to sign the RBA-related commitment letters. For
better supply chain management, Lotes conducts yearly audits and regular business reviews
and holds the yearly supplier conference as measures. In 2025, Lotes conducted written
evaluations of 110 suppliers and on-site audits of 61 suppliers. In addition, all 76 newly added
suppliers passed its environmental and social responsibility evaluations. Lotes has required new
suppliers to sign the "Supplier Social and Environmental Responsibility Commitment Letter"
since 2010 to extend social responsibility concepts and requirements to the supply chain.
For "green" purchase management, Lotes has adopted the IECQ QC 080000 hazardous
substance process management system. Lotes asks its suppliers to follow the RoHS, HSF, and
REACH guidelines to avoid using hazardous materials. Lotes also conducts conflict minerals due
diligence and requires suppliers to sign non-conflict mineral declarations and complete surveys
tracing mineral sources to smelters. Overall, Lotes believes it has constructed a comprehensive
system covering system certification (e.g., ISO 9001), product design, supply chain
management, and production techniques to ensure that the entire manufacturing process, from
raw materials to end products, follows green principles. All products from Lotes are now 100%
compliant with RoHS standards.
S Data security 2
Lotes has established a team to implement data security regulations, planning and maintenance.
Moreover, it monitors vulnerabilities in its information systems each year through regular
vulnerability scanning, access control reviews and information security audits. The company
reported no losses resulting from information security incidents in 2025.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

Greater China Technology Semiconductors
Asia Pacific
Industry View Attractive
Limited direct impact on Aspeed and Montage in the near term,

but we see potential long-term demand risks.
The US BIS has tightened export controls to restrict Chinese companies' remote
access to advanced computing capacity through third-country data centers, while
adding licensing requirements for advanced chips shipped to key Southeast Asian
markets, including Singapore and Malaysia (link).
Our view: Gary Yu (China Internet Analyst; link) sees this as another step toward
closing loopholes in Al compute access, potentially slowing GPU deployment by

China-linked cloud operators and raising compliance costs for overseas Al
infrastructure investment. New supplies of advanced chipsets within China to
become increasingly limited. For cloud semis, we see limited direct impact on
Aspeed and Montage in the near term but there are potential long-term demand
risks. Aspeed has a high share in GPU and China server platforms. Montage
interface and retimer chips may also be impacted over the long term.
Please also refer to our recent notes: Cloud Semis: Peak Growth, Less Upside;

Investor pushback on our cloud semis/old memory downgrades.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

產業速報

1 群益投顧 
精材(3374 TT)與 台 積 電 (2330 TT)簽 訂 先 進 封 裝 oS 產 能 外 包 協 議 ， 深 化 策 略 合 作 。 精
材 此 次 取 得 台 積 電 CoWoS 後段 oS 產 能 外 包 合 作 ， 對 長 期 業 務 結 構 升 級 具 正 面 意 義 ，
惟 股 價 於 公 告 前 已 連 續 6 個 交 易 日 上 漲 ， 累 計 漲 幅 約 40%，11/13/2026 收盤達 519 元
漲 停 ， 本 益 比 已 達 43 倍 ， 加 上 新 產 線 預 計 2029 年才開始第一期量產， 較 市 場 預 期
2027~2028 年量產晚， 短 期 獲 利 貢 獻 有 限 ， 且 仍 有 設 備 資 本 支 出 、 折 舊 及 CB 潛在稀
釋 風 險 ， 因 此 短 線 不 宜 追 價 。

研 究 員 ： 陳 俐 妍   
ly.c@capital.com.tw

台 積 電 擴 大 先 進 封 裝 外 包 布 局 ， 精 材 預 計 2029 年切入 CoWoS 後段 oS 製
程 ：  
精材(3374 TT) 於 11/13/2026 公 告 董 事 會 決 議 通 過 與 具 重 大 影 響 之 投 資 者 台
積電(2330 TT) 簽 訂 產 能 外 包 協 議 ， 契 約 期 間 自 2026/11/13 至 2033/12/31，
雙 方 將 針 對 先 進 封 裝 後 段 製 程 展 開 長 期 策 略 合 作。根 據 公 告 內 容，精 材 計 畫
進駐台積電中部科學園區廠房建置產線並負責生產營運，由台積電提供廠
房空間及先進封裝技術與製造能力支援，精材則自行投入生產設備及自動
物 料 搬 運 系 統 (AMHS)資 本 支 出，切 入 先 進 封 裝 供 應 鏈 中 的 oS(on-Substrate)
模 組 。 該 產 線 規 劃 於 2029 年 實 現 第 一 期 量 產 ， 雙 方 並 將 共 同 確 保 新 產 線 的
技 術 規 範、品 質 及 良 率 符 合 台 積 電 與 客 戶 要 求。此 外，精 材 董 事 會 同 步 通 過
發 行 新 台 幣 150 億 元 可 轉 換 公 司 債 (CB)， 以 支 應 初 期 產 能 建 置 需 求 。  
既 有 台 積 電 合 作 關 係 延 伸 至 先 進 封 裝 ， 有 助 精 材 拓 展 AI 及 HPC 業務：  
就 精 材 本 身 而 言，此 次 合 作 是 既 有 台 積 電 供 應 鏈 關 係 的 深 化。精 材 原 以 CIS
封 裝 及 晶 圓 級 封 裝 (WLCSP) 為 主 要 業 務，並 承 接 台 積 電 晶 圓 測 試 (CP)外 包 訂
單 ， 惟 既 有 測 試 機 台 於 08/2026 已 達 滿 載 ， 且 受 限 於 無 新 增 無 塵 室 空 間 ， 部
分 台 積 電 測 試 訂 單 轉 由 欣 銓 (3264 TT)承 接。此 次 透 過 進 駐 台 積 電 中 科 廠 區，
精材得以突破自身廠房空間限制，免除購地建廠所需的前置時間與土地成
本，並 利 用 台 積 電 既 有 廠 區 及 技 術 支 援，降 低 新 產 線 建 置 與 製 程 導 入 的 部 分
門 檻。由 於 台 積 電 目 前 持 有 精 材 約 41%股 權，雙 方 已 有 長 期 合 作 基 礎，此 次
進 一 步 由 晶 圓 測 試 及 既 有 封 裝 業 務 延 伸 至 CoWoS 後段 oS 製 程 ， 有 助 精 材
擴 大 與 台 積 電 的 合 作 範 圍，並 提 高 未 來 AI 及 HPC 相 關 業 務 的 營 收 占 比。不
過，CP 測 試 與 oS 屬 不 同 製 程，仍 須 觀 察 新 產 線 設 備 裝 機、製 程 驗 證 及 良 率
爬 升 進 度 。  
台 積 電 擴 大 先 進 封 裝 供 應 鏈 分 工 ， Interposer 與 oS 同 步 建 立 外 部 產 能 ：  
此 次 精 材 切 入 CoWoS 後段 oS 製 程 ， 應 放 在 台 積 電 持 續 擴 大 先 進 封 裝 供 應
鏈 分 工 的 整 體 策 略 下 觀 察 。 隨 AI 與 HPC 晶片朝 Chiplet、 HBM 及 異 質 整 合
發展，CoWoS 不 同 製 程 環 節 均 面 臨 產 能 擴 充 需 求 。 台 積 電 除 持 續 擴 充 自 身
先 進 封 裝 產 能 外，亦 逐 步 透 過 外 部 合 作 夥 伴 增 加 不 同 環 節 的 供 應 能 力，例 如
Interposer 方 面 與 GlobalFoundries 合 作 建 立 外 部 供 應 來 源，並 透 過 世 界 先 進
等 業 者 擴 充 相 關 產 能；後 段 先 進 封 裝 則 持 續 擴 大 與 OSAT 的 合 作，此 次 再 由
持股約 41%的 精 材 規 劃 於 台 積 電 中 科 廠 區 建 置 oS 產 線 ， 顯 示 台 積 電 正 逐 步
建 立 結 合 關 係 企 業 與 外 部 供 應 商 的 先 進 封 裝 供 應 鏈，以 提 高 產 能 配 置 彈 性。 
不 同 製 程 環 節 的 外 包 條 件 與 策 略 並 不 相 同 。 Interposer 外 包 主 要 在 於 增 加 供
應 來 源 及 產 能 彈 性；CoWoS 則 可 區 分 為 CoW 與 oS 兩 個 階 段，其 中 CoW 涉
及 晶 圓、矽 中 介 層 及 晶 片 間 的 高 精 度 整 合，製 程 複 雜 度 與 良 率 要 求 較 高，外

### Page 2

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 11 月 16 日

2 群益投顧 
包 仍 受 技 術 及 良 率 條 件 限 制；相 較 之 下，oS 主 要 負 責 將 完 成 的 CoW 模 組 接
合 至 基 板，隨 OSAT 業 者 製 程 經 驗 累 積 及 良 率 逐 步 成 熟，已 成 為 較 適 合 擴 大
委 外 的 環 節 ， 目 前 台 積 電 CoWoS 後段 oS 製 程 已 大 量 採 用 外 包 模 式 。 由 於
OSAT 的 先 進 封 裝 毛 利 率 通 常 低 於 台 積 電 整 體 製 造 業 務 ， 將 部 分 oS 產 能 交
由 外 部 業 者 承 接，亦 有 助 台 積 電 將 有 限 的 無 塵 室 空 間、設 備 及 工 程 資 源 優 先
配 置 於 技 術 門 檻 較 高 的 CoW 及 次 世 代 先 進 封 裝 製 程 。  
精材 2029 年 才 量 產 ， 2026～ 2028 年 既 有 OSAT 仍 持 續 受 惠 AI 封 裝 需 求 ：  
精 材 新 產 線 預 計 2029 年 才 開 始 第 一 期 量 產，因 此 在 2026～ 2028 年 間，並 不
會立即改變既有封測業者的供需格局。台積電雖可依製程整合需求向客戶
建 議 合 作 封 測 廠，但 客 戶 仍 可 依 自 身 需 求 直 接 與 封 測 業 者 洽 談 產 能 及 合 作，
因 此 先 進 封 裝 訂 單 並 非 完 全 由 台 積 電 單 方 面 分 配。在 AI/HPC 晶 片 持 續 推 動
先 進 封 裝 規 格 升 級 下，日 月 光 投 控、力 成 及 其 他 既 有 封 測 業 者 仍 可 持 續 受 惠
於新增外包需求，並透過製程改善、良率提升及設備效率優化增加有效產
出。 
至 2029 年 後 ， 精 材 oS 新 產 線 及 其 他 業 者 新 增 產 能 將 陸 續 開 出 ， 加 上 既 有
OSAT 持 續 透 過 良 率 改 善 增 加 有 效 產 能 ， 供 應 鏈 競 爭 可 能 逐 步 加 劇 。 因 此 ，
中 長 期 仍 須 觀 察 AI/HPC 需 求 成 長 能 否 持 續 吸 收 新 增 設 備 及 良 率 提 升 所 帶
來的有效供給，這將是影響先進封裝產業供需及各業者獲利表現的重要變
數。 
2029 年 後 新 增 有 效 產 能 可 能 增 加 ， 精 材 仍 面 臨 資 本 支 出 與 股 本 稀 釋 壓 力 ：  
雖然 AI/HPC 晶 片 持 續 朝 先 進 製 程、Chiplet 及 異 質 整 合 發 展，將 支 撐 先 進 封
裝 長 期 需 求 ， 但 目 前 CoWoS 供 不 應 求 ， 除 AI/HPC 需 求 快 速 成 長 、 晶 片 封
裝 面 積 擴 大 及 HBM 整 合 增 加 外 ， 製 程 複 雜 度 高 、 良 率 尚 待 提 升 ， 亦 可 能 限
制 有 效 產 能 釋 出。值 得 留 意 的 是，隨 製 程 技 術 持 續 改 進、設 備 調 校 及 量 產 經
驗 累 積，既 有 產 線 良 率 若 逐 步 提 升，即 使 未 新 增 設 備，也 可 能 增 加 有 效 產 出；
若 再 加 上 台 積 電 及 全 球 OSAT 業 者 持 續 擴 充 產 能 ， 未 來 供 給 增 幅 可 能 高 於
市 場 目 前 預 期 。 因 此 ， 現 階 段 CoWoS 供 不 應 求 未 必 能 長 期 延 續 ， 若 良 率 瓶
頸 逐 步 突 破，供 需 可 能 由 緊 俏 轉 向 平 衡，甚 至 出 現 供 給 較 為 充 裕 的 情 況，後
續 仍 須 觀 察 AI/HPC 需 求 成 長 能 否 吸 收 新 增 設 備 及 良 率 改 善 所 帶 來 的 有 效
供給。  
此 外 ， 精 材 須 自 行 負 擔 設 備 及 AMHS 資 本 支 出 ， 設 備 投 資 至 新 產 線 營 收 貢
獻 仍 有 時 間 落 差，設 備 陸 續 投 入 使 用 後 亦 將 增 加 折 舊 費 用，若 初 期 產 能 利 用
率 及 良 率 爬 升 不 如 預 期，可 能 對 毛 利 率 及 營 業 利 益 形 成 壓 力。資 金 方 面，精
材截至 11/2026 股 本 為 27.14 億 元 ， 約 當 2.714 億 股 ， 預 計 發 行 150 億元
CB， 目 前 尚 未 確 定 轉 換 價 格 ； 若 以 11/13/2026 收 盤 價 519 元 作 為 假 設 轉 換
價格，並以全數轉換估算，新增股數約 2,890 萬 股 ， 相 較 目 前 股 數 增 加 約
10.65%， 轉 換 後 原 股 東 持 股 比 例 將 稀 釋 約 9.63%。 惟 此 為 假 設 情 境 ， 實 際 稀
釋 程 度 仍 須 視 最 終 發 行 金 額、轉 換 價 格 及 轉 換 條 件 而 定。因 此，此 次 合 作 雖
有助精材長期業務結構升級，但仍須考量資本支出回收效益、折舊吸收能
力、CB 潛 在 稀 釋 及 未 來 產 業 供 需 變 化 ， 不 宜 直 接 以 目 前 CoWoS 供 不 應 求
的 狀 況 外 推 未 來 獲 利 。

3 群益投顧 
精 材 短 線 評 價 已 反 映 部 分 成 長 預 期 ， 封 測 股 則 可 留 意 修 正 後 的 布 局 機 會 ：  
精 材 此 次 取 得 台 積 電 CoWoS 後段 oS 產 能 外 包 合 作 ， 對 長 期 業 務 結 構 升 級
具 正 面 意 義 ， 惟 股 價 於 公 告 前 已 連 續 6 個 交 易 日 上 漲 ， 累 計 漲 幅 約 40%，
11/13/2026 收 盤 達 519 元 漲 停，本 益 比 已 達 43 倍，加 上 新 產 線 預 計 2029 年
才 開 始 第 一 期 量 產 ， 短 期 獲 利 貢 獻 有 限 ， 且 仍 有 設 備 資 本 支 出 、 折 舊 及 CB
潛 在 稀 釋 風 險 ， 因 此 短 線 不 宜 追 價 。  
另一方面，不宜因精材取得台積電合作，就立即下修日月光投控(3711 TT)
及力成(6239 TT) 的 中 短 期 投 資 看 法 。 2026 年至 2028 年 精 材 新 產 線 尚 未 量
產，既 有 封 測 廠 仍 可 持 續 承 接 AI 及 HPC 先 進 封 裝 與 測 試 外 溢 訂 單，並 透 過
產 線 效 率 及 良 率 改 善 提 升 有 效 產 出，中 短 期 應 以 訂 單 能 見 度、產 能 利 用 率 及
獲 利 表 現 作 為 主 要 觀 察 重 點。4Q26 以 來，部 分 封 測 股 已 因 市 場 對 AI 資 本 支
出 及 投 資 效 益 的 疑 慮 而 大 幅 修 正，若 後 續 訂 單 及 營 運 展 望 未 明 顯 轉 弱，評 價
修正反而可能提供布局機會，可持續留意受惠先進封裝需求的日月光投控
(3711 TT) 、力成(6239 TT)， 以 及 承 接 晶 圓 測 試 外 溢 需 求 的 欣 銓 (3264 TT)。
至 2029 年 後 ， 隨 精 材 及 其 他 業 者 新 增 產 能 陸 續 開 出 ， 加 上 既 有 產 線 良 率 提
升 帶 動 有 效 供 給 增 加 ， 則 須 留 意 供 需 變 化 及 競 爭 加 劇 風 險 。

### Page 4

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 11 月 16 日

4 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】  
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 ≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15%≤首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 <35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 <15%

中立(Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等  
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整  
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】  
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較 3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為 四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等 係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。   
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」 及 「 區 間 操 作 」 均有 upside 目 標 價 。 差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。  
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ：  
1. 達 目 標 價 。  
2. 雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。  
3. 推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 ， 亦 將 出 具 降 評 報 告 。

【免責聲明】 
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

두산에너빌리티 (034020)

EARNINGS REVIEW Company Analysis | 건설/원전 | 2026. 10. 30  
숫자로 증명하는 중

3Q26 Review: 에너빌부문 믹스개선, 두산밥캣 호실적 
두산에너빌리티 3분기 매출액은 4조 4,494억원으로 전년동기대비 14.7% 증가, 컨센
서스에 부합했다. 영업이익은 2,612억원으로 전년동기대비 90.6% 증가, 컨센서스 영
업이익 2,464억원 대비 6.0% 상회하는 호실적을 기록했다. 에너빌리티부문의 원자
력, 가스터빈 매출 증가에 따른 믹스 개선 효과와 두산밥캣의 가격정책 효과에 힘입은 
마진 개선으로 안정적인 실적 기조가 이어지고 있어 긍정적이다. 분기 환평가손실로 
인해 순이익은 여타 수출기업과 유사하게 컨센서스 대비 하회했다. 에너빌부문의 수주
는 누계 10조원으로 목표대비 75%를 채웠으며, 수주 대기중인 안건들을 고려할 때 
당분간 수주 업사이클이 몇년간 지속될 것임이 분명한 시점이다. 
기회가 아닌 경쟁력, 모멘텀이 아닌 수주  
두산에너빌리티에 대해 투자의견 Buy 유지하나, 목표주가를 95,000원으로 하향했다. 
목표 EBITDA는 2035년 EBITDA에 WACC 8%를 적용, 목표 EV/EBITDA는 Global 
Peers인 GE Vernova의 2026년 EV/EBITDA 멀티플에서 자회사 실적 변동성을 고
려해 10% 할인 적용했다. 호실적과 수주 증가에도 GEV의 멀티플 감소와 순차입금 
증가로 인한 NAV 감소를 밸류에이션에 반영한 결과이다. 전일 주가는 한수원의 체코 
원전 시공사 협상 결렬로 인해 7.65% 하락 마감했다. 시공 수주는 아쉽지만 두산에너
빌리티의 체코 원전 기자재 납품에는 전혀 영향이 없으며, 글로벌 전력 수요 증가로 경
제적해자인 대형 가스 터빈 수급 쇼티지는 당분간 지속될 전망임을 고려할 때 전일 주
가는 낙폭 과대로 판단한다. 1) 두산에너빌리티의 가스터빈은 이미 2031년 출하 물량
에 대한 예약 주문이 마무리되고 있으며, 2) 또한 AP1000의 우량한 수주 파이프라인
과, 3) 뉴스케일파워, 테라파워, 롤스로이스, X Energy 등의 SMR 제작을 위한 협업 역
시 지속되고 있다는 점에 주목한다. 전일 기사를 통해 뉴스케일파워 SMR 1호 제작 계
약을 체결했다는 소식을 전했으며, 이제 에너지 트렌지션은 기회가 아닌 회사의 경쟁
력으로, 가스터빈과 원전은 모멘텀이 아니라 수주로 입증되고 있다.  
 Analyst 김세련 
sally.kim@ls-sec.co.kr

Document 257: A primer on fiscal measures debated in French elections_ Most candidates commit to restoring fiscal sustainability, with uneven credibility (2026-11-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/A primer on fiscal measures debated in French elections_ Most candidates commit to restoring fiscal sustainability, with uneven credibility (2026-11-23).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-11-23
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
7
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.43 / 23 November 2026FOCUS — FRENCH ELECTIONS
A PRIMER ON FISCAL MEASURES DEBATED IN FRENCH ELECTIONS: MOST CANDIDATES COMMIT TO RESTORING FISCAL
SUSTAINABILITY, WITH UNEVEN CREDIBILITY
1 Other growth-critical policy proposals will be reviewed in thematic deep-dives to be published over the coming months.
This note offers an overview of the measures proposed by presidential election candidates from the main political groups,

namely Marine Le Pen, Jean-Luc Mélenchon, Édouard Philippe, Raphaël Glucksmann, David Lisnard and Bruno Retailleau.
The focus here is on their proposals for public finances and pensions 1. Overall, budget plans go in the right direction but
rely on overly optimistic costs and revenue assumptions. However, in several cases a proposed “golden rule” enhances
credibility. The proposals regarding pensions improve on the status quo in most cases, including, in some cases, by intro-

ducing a capitalisation pillar. However, most safeguard costly features of the current system, without enough measures to
offset their impact fully.
KEY TAKEAWAYS
- The bulk of the candidates target a below 3% of GDP fiscal deficit by 2032. The budget proposals are often optimistic (or lack sufficient detail), which is a
common feature of previous presidential campaigns (e.g., in 2017, E. Macron proposed reducing public spending to 50% of GDP by 2022).

- A majority of candidates propose a “golden rule”, including the National Rally (Rassemblement national). A rule would lend credibility to their fiscal path (if
the planned measures are insufficient, the golden rule would require them to adopt additional measures, as it was done e.g. in Germany under the debt brake).
- The budget announcements made by several candidates — including the National Rally (RN) and La France Insoumise (LFI) — are expected to worsen the

fiscal deficit in 2027. According to our calculations, a one-year delay in fiscal consolidation would result in a 2 pp increase in the public debt-to-GDP ratio
by the end of that consolidation (in 2032, debt would reach 126.5% of GDP instead of 124.5% without this delay), and even more if the deficit or interest rate
conditions were to deteriorate significantly.

### Page 2

8
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.43 / 23 November 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
- In the absence of a golden rule, LFI’s commitment to fiscal consolidation remains uncertain. The candidate’s promises are very costly, leading to a clear in-
crease in the fiscal deficit. Some of the proposed measures are confiscatory in nature and would likely shrink the tax base. The candidate proposes no binding
safeguards to ensure that the deficit does not spiral out of control. Furthermore, his proposal to write off part of the public debt held by the ECB could be

interpreted as a way to avoid having to undertake fiscal consolidation efforts to stabilise public debt (not to mention its feasibility).
KEY TAKEAWAYS
- The candidates’ positions on pensions reflect real, underlying progress, particularly in order to mitigate the impact of the repeal of the Borne reform (retire-
ment age back to 62 years old) through savings on other parameters (contribution period, or tightened conditions such as an increased discount per missing

quarter).
- The prospect of fully indexing pensions, as proposed in several candidates' platforms, would be very costly (nearly 2% of GDP).
- Both measures (lowering the retirement age and full indexation) undermine the system’s long-term financial stability. Everything else equal, they also
reduce the size of the active labour force, a headwind to growth and fiscal sustainability.
- Édouard Philippe, David Lisnard and Bruno Retailleau advocate raising the retirement age to 65, and Édouard Philippe also calls for extending the contribu-

tion period to 45 years (while maintaining more favourable treatment for long careers and physically demanding jobs). The savings associated with extending
working careers represent a significant contribution to the announced fiscal consolidation effort.
- With regard to capitalisation, there are marked differences between the candidates who currently rule it out (J.L. Mélenchon, R. Glucksmann) and the others,
who have all made proposals along these lines (including the RN).

entering the workforce.

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX tăng điểm trong tuần qua, +68.24 điểm tương ứng +4.00%, với thanh khoản cải 
thiện. Thiết bị điện, Khách sạn - Nhà hàng và Thiết bị dịch vụ y tế là các nhóm ngành có 
mức tăng rõ rệt nhất trong tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần 
trước với giá trị -966 tỷ đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, ACB và VCB; mặt khác, tự doanh duy 
trì mua ròng ở mức 137 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều mua ròng 1,335 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua nhưng đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -3,049 hợp đồng. Tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng 
1,815 hợp đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -6,000 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam hồi phục trở lại sau bốn tuần giảm liên tiếp, khi VN-Index bật tăng mạnh 
từ vùng hỗ trợ quanh 1.700 điểm với thanh khoản cải thiện. Sau nhịp điều chỉnh kéo dài 
khiến mặt bằng định giá về vùng hấp dẫn, lực cầu bắt đáy đã nhập cuộc quyết liệt hơn, 
với phiên 21/10 tăng gần 47 điểm — một trong những phiên tăng mạnh nhất nhiều tháng 
gần đây. Điểm tích cực và đáng chú ý nhất là khối ngoại đã xuất hiện hai phiên đảo chiều 
mua ròng trở lại sau khoảng 20 phiên bán ròng liên tiếp, tập trung vào nhóm Bluechip 
như VIC, TCB, VHM, MSN — tín hiệu đầu tiên cho thấy áp lực rút vốn ngoại có thể đang dần 
hạ nhiệt. Thông tin tích cực tác động lên thị trường đến từ các chỉ đạo của NHNN và Chính 
phủ về việc hạ mặt bằng lãi suất để hỗ trợ tăng trưởng kinh tế. Bên cạnh đó, mùa báo cáo 
KQKD quý 3 khả quan đang diễn ra cũng là yếu tố hỗ trợ xu hướng thị trường chung.

19/10/2026

Nguồn: V_KBSource

Triển vọng kinh tế 4Q2026 
Giữ nhịp tăng trưởng, vững vàng về itch

Một số dự báo chính của KBSV về kinh tế Việt Nam trong năm 2026:

1) Tăng trưởng GDP năm 2026 được dự báo sẽ đạt 9.0%, với động lực chính 
đến từ định hướng đẩy mạnh giải ngân đầu tư công của Chính phủ, dòng 
vốn FDI duy trì tích cực và hoạt động sản xuất công nghiệp phục hồi theo 
xuất khẩu. Về rủi ro, chúng tôi cho rằng Việt Nam sẽ tiếp tục đối mặt với 
một số yếu tố bất định từ bên ngoài, bao gồm: rủi ro xung đột Mỹ – Iran 
kéo dài hơn dự kiến, xu hướng tăng của mặt bằng lãi suất toàn cầu gây gia 
tăng áp lực tài chính và kéo giảm tổng cầu, cùng nguy cơ vỡ bong bóng AI.

2) Chúng tôi nâng dự báo lạm phát bình quân năm 2026 của Việt Nam lên 
mức 4.7% YoY, cao hơn mục tiêu kiểm soát lạm phát của Chính phủ, dựa 
trên kịch bản dự báo thận trọng hơn về diễn biến xung đột địa chính trị và 
giá năng lượng thế giới trong ngắn hạn.

3) Mặt bằng lãi suất trong quý IV được dự báo sẽ ổn định quanh vùng hiện 
tại và không xuất hiện thêm nhịp tăng đột biến, nhờ dòng tiền cải thiện 
từ giải ngân đầu tư công và kỳ vọng có thêm các biện pháp hỗ trợ thanh 
khoản trung dài hạn từ phía Chính phủ và NHNN.

4) Tỷ giá USD/VND trong quý cuối năm có thể chịu một số sức ép do Fed dự 
báo còn thêm một lần tăng lãi suất nữa trong năm. Dù vậy, chúng tôi cho 
rằng tỷ giá USD/VND khó tăng mạnh mà chỉ quay lại mức đầu năm, dựa 
trên kỳ vọng chỉ số DXY không biến động mạnh, nguồn cung ngoại tệ cải 
thiện (FDI giải ngân, kiều hối, nâng hạng TTCK) và chênh lệch lãi suất 
VND–USD duy trì trạng thái dương.

Tham khảo nội dung chi tiết trong báo cáo tại đây.

### Page 4

21/10/2026

CTCP VÀNG BẠC ĐÁ QUÝ PHÚ NHUẬN (PNJ)  
Cập nhật ĐHĐCĐ bất thường 21/10/2026

Tái cơ cấu để xử lý áp lực thanh khoản  
— ĐHĐCĐ bất thường PNJ ngày 21/10/2026 đã thông qua điều chỉnh kế hoạch kinh 
doanh và phương án phát hành riêng lẻ để bổ sung vốn tái cấu trúc. Ban lãnh đạo đặt 
ra các vấn đề trọng yếu là đáp ứng nhu cầu thanh khoản và chuẩn bị nguồn lực để thực 
hiện các cam kết thu đổi đã tích lũy trong quá khứ. Bên cạnh đó, các cổ đông tham dự 
cũng đặt các câu hỏi liên quan đến tình hình hoạt động qua các điểm chính (1) dòng 
tiền hoạt động kinh doanh; (2) diễn biến thị trường sau khi PNJ áp dụng chính sách thu 
đổi mới; (3) hiệu quả hoạt động sau tái cấu trúc.

Nội dung chính tại đại hội 
— Theo Ban lãnh đạo, thông tin liên quan đến P-Lab từ đầu tháng 7 khiến khách hàng gia 
tăng bán lại sản phẩm. Tổng giá trị thu đổi và mua lại trong tháng đạt gần 6,000 tỷ VND, 
so với khoảng 7,000 tỷ VND trong cả năm 2025. Theo BCTC riêng, nguồn tiền khả dụng 
giảm từ khoảng 3,700 tỷ VND cuối Q2/2026 xuống 770 tỷ VND cuối Q3/2026, đã bao 
gồm khoản vay 700 tỷ VND từ Chủ tịch HĐQT. Tỷ trọng kim cương và trang sức kim 
cương trong tổng tồn kho tăng từ 41% lên 52%. 
— Q3/2026 ghi nhận lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND trước phần tăng trích lập dự phòng. Để giảm 
áp lực thanh toán, PNJ kéo dài thời gian chi trả đối với kim cương lên 120 ngày từ tháng 
07/2026. Thay đổi chính sách cũng ảnh hưởng đến niềm tin khách hàng và sức mua, cụ 
thể doanh thu và lợi nhuận gộp Q3/2026 đạt lần lượt 6,995/609 tỷ VND, giảm 14%/62% 
YoY. Chi phí bán hàng và quản lý giảm 13% YoY chưa đủ bù đắp mức giảm lợi nhuận 
gộp, khiến PNJ lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND, so với lãi 496 tỷ VND trong Q3/2025. 
— PNJ nâng giả định tỷ lệ thu đổi trên giá trị thu đổi còn lại lên 80%, so với 28.8% đối với 
nhóm sản phẩm giai đoạn 2020–2026 trong ước tính tại BCTC bán niên 2026. Tổng dự 
phòng tổn thất dự kiến tăng từ 2,267 tỷ VND lên 7,071 tỷ VND. Kế hoạch điều chỉnh ghi 
nhận doanh thu 39,057 tỷ VND, lỗ trước thuế 6,059 tỷ VND và lỗ sau thuế 6,271 tỷ VND. 
Theo Ban lãnh đạo, LNST loại trừ dự phòng ước khoảng 800 tỷ VND, chủ yếu nhờ kết 
quả Q1/2026. Khoản dự phòng phản ánh tổn thất ước tính cho toàn bộ kịch bản, không 
đồng nghĩa toàn bộ giá trị sẽ được chi trả ngay trong kỳ. 
— Giá trị cam kết thu đổi tích lũy đối với kim cương và trang sức kim cương tại cuối 
Q3/2026 được PNJ ước tính khoảng 21,000 tỷ VND. Với hàng đã bán, doanh nghiệp dự 
kiến chuyển đổi quyền lợi khách hàng sang sản phẩm và các chương trình ưu đãi. Với 
sản phẩm bán mới, chính sách thu đổi định hướng theo giá thị trường tại thời điểm 
giao dịch, thay cho tỷ lệ cố định trên giá trị hóa đơn, nhằm hạn chế tiếp tục tích lũy 
nghĩa vụ tiềm tàng trong tương lai theo cơ chế cũ. 
Tham khảo nội dung chi tiết trong báo cáo tại đây.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX hình thành cây nến xanh thân dài trên khung tuần và đáng chú ý là cho phản ứng 
bật tăng ngay tại cạnh dưới của mẫu hình tam giác đi ngang trung hạn, cho thấy tín hiệu 
tích cực đã quay trở lại với xu hướng ngắn hạn. Trên khung ngày, sau 2 phiên điều chỉnh, 
rung lắc vào đầu tuần, chỉ số cũng đảo chiều tăng điểm khá dốc, với 3 cây nến xanh liên 
tiếp với thanh khoản được tăng cường, củng cố thêm tín hiệu xác nhận cho đà hồi phục. 
Sau nhịp bật tăng mạnh từ đáy, VNINDEX được dự báo sẽ sớm gặp phải áp lực điều chỉnh 
khi quay lên điểm giữa của dải đi ngang theo mẫu hình tam giác, tương ứng vùng kháng cự 
kế tiếp tại quanh 1810. Tuy nhiên, xu hướng ngắn hạn vẫn có phần nghiêng về chiều tăng, 
với tín hiệu xác nhận đáy của một số mã cổ phiếu sau quá trình sụt giảm kéo dài.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
CONSUMER PANEL Q3 2026 
MORE RESILIENCE
3 November 2026
• Average consumer spend across 
the Westpac-DataX Consumer 
Panel remained resilient, rising 
1.5% in Q3 and 4.6%yr. 
• While some of this reflected a price-
driven surge in fuel spend (+0.3%qtr) 
discretionary categories also posted 
robust gains (contributing 0.9ppts 
to the headline rise) with non-fuel 
essential categories contributing 
positively as well (+0.5ptps).
• Households continued to add to savings 
and buffers. Savings rose 1.3%qtr, led 
by younger households and mortgagors, 
with average savings buffers increasing 
to 22.2 months of mortgage repayments. 
• The detail shows financial pressures 
remain uneven but spending patterns 
appear to be converging across 
consumers with low, medium and high 
buffers. Much of this appears to relate 
to a reduction in fuel consumption.

Westpac–DataX Consumer Panel
The Westpac–DataX Consumer Panel is a large 
dataset that gives a timely and detailed picture 
of Australian consumer finances and behaviour. 
Developed by DataX, Westpac’s data analytics 
team, the dataset links transaction activity with 
balance sheet information to give a complete view 
of income, spending, saving and borrowing flows. 
All data is de-identified and aggregated to 
ensure privacy. The resulting sample of over 
one million customers gives an accurate 
representation of trends across the wider 
Australian consumer and is perfectly suited 
to quickly tracing responses to events like 
interest rate moves and energy price shocks.
Powered by 
Fuel and non-fuel spending lift

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Heatmap of monthly spending categories
The Westpac DataX Consumer Panel shows average
consumer spending remained resilient in Q3, rising 1.5%
despite a further lift in fuel costs. Fuel contributed 0.3ppts
to quarterly growth, but discretionary spending did more

of the heavy lifting, contributing 0.9ppts as recreation
and travel-related spending recovered. Households also
continued to add to their financial buffers: savings up 1.3%
in the quarter and average buffers across households with
a mortgage increasing to 22.2 months of repayments.
Beneath the aggregate picture, lower-buffer households
remain more exposed to higher fuel costs and mortgage
repayments, but their discretionary spending share is
looking increasingly similar to other households.

Spending holds up despite higher fuel costs
Average household spending increased 1.5% in Q3, with much
of the strength again driven by transport-related spending.
Fuel contributed 0.3ppts to quarterly growth, while transport
excluding fuel contributed a further 0.4ppts. Excluding the
direct effects of the price-driven lift in fuel, average spend
was up 1.2%qtr. Importantly, stronger fuel spending did not
come at the expense of discretionary purchases. Discretionary

categories contributed 0.9ppts to quarterly spending growth,
compared with 0.5ppts from non-fuel essentials.
Solid rise in non-fuel spend and financial buffers
Discretionary spending dominates
Recreation and culture contributed 0.3ppts to growth, reflecting
stronger spending on sporting services, while restaurants and
hotels added a further 0.2ppts. Transport spending was also
boosted by a rebound in airline expenditure, which more than
unwound the weakness recorded in Q2 when refunds relating

to cancellations due to conflict in the Middle East weighed on
the category. Discretionary spending rose 0.9%qtr, following a
flat result and a 0.1% rise in the previous two quarters.
Illiana Jain
Economist, Westpac Group
P: +61 403  908 032
E: illiana.jain@westpac.com.au

### Page 3

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts are based are reasonable, 
the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
Savings growth by mortgage status
Households continue to rebuild savings
Savings increased 1.3% in Q3, with the strongest gains again 
recorded among younger households. Savings balances for 
18–34 year olds increased 2.0%qtr, compared with 0.7% 
for those aged 55 and over. Mortgagors also continued to 
accumulate savings more rapidly, recording a 2.8% increase 
compared with 0.7% among non-mortgagors. Savings growth 
among mortgagors has now outpaced non-mortgagors for 
three consecutive years.
Stronger savings have continued to lift mortgage buffers. 
Average buffers (savings divided by the sum of essential 
expenses and minimum mortgage repayments), increased to 
22.2 months of repayments in September. Looking within the 
unit-record sample, median mortgage buffers jumped from 
6.3 months in August to 6.8 months, its highest level since the 
series began in 2019.

Median mortgage buffers
Fuel spending a clear drag on consumers
Fuel spending as a share of total spending
Spending outcomes continued to vary across age groups. 
The squeeze remains most apparent among younger 
households. Fuel spending rose 5.1%qtr for 18–34 year olds, 
against a 1.5%qtr increase in non-fuel spending. By contrast, 
non-fuel spending increased 2.1%qtr among households aged 
55 and over. 
The gap between mortgagors and non-mortgagors was 
more modest. Spending rose 1.6%qtr among mortgagors 
and 1.9%qtr among those without a mortgage, suggesting 
mortgage status alone masks considerable variation in 
household financial positions – a divide that becomes clearer 
when households are grouped by the size of their mortgage 
buffers (see discussion further on in this note). Note that ‘non 
mortgagors’ covers a diverse range of consumers including 
those that are renting and those that own their homes without 
a mortgage.

### Page 4

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts are based are reasonable, 
the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
Median litres of fuel consumed
More importantly, their consumption appears to be less 
responsive to price movements. During periods of cheaper 
fuel, high-buffer households show a noticeably larger lift in 
litres purchased, consistent with stockpiling when prices are 
favourable. Low-buffer households show much less of this 
response, while their fuel volumes have also adjusted less 
during the recent increase in fuel costs.
The spending data show the consequence. Fuel expenditure 
has risen sharply across all three groups recently, but remains 
highest among low-buffer households, where it also absorbs 
a larger share of total spending. Food spending has also 
declined since 2023. This may partly reflect households 
economising within the category, including switching towards 
cheaper or home-brand products, although further work is 
needed to test this.
Taken together, the households with the smallest savings 
cushions face higher mortgage repayments, spend more 
on fuel and appear to have less scope to adjust their fuel 
consumption when prices change. 
Discretionary spend as a share of total
This suggests that in aggregate, household balance sheets 
are looking healthy. The picture is a little at odds with the very 
weak reads on our Westpac-Melbourne Index Consumer 
Sentiment Index which suggests finances are coming under 
much more pressure. The fact that both average spending and 
saving both rose suggests incomes over the quarter were not 
overly stretched and that more of the weakness in sentiment 
may relate to concerns about the outlook (something that may 
also be encouraging consumers to build buffers now, while they 
still can).
Pressures intense for low-buffer households
Aggregate buffer measures mask sizeable differences across 
mortgagors. We can get a sense of these variations by 
combining mortgagors into three broad groups according to 
buffer size, i.e. low, middle and high buffer groups. This shows 
that households with the smallest buffers are also more 
exposed to both interest rates and fuel costs.

The buffer differences are relatively stark, the median savings 
of the low-buffer group just $2k compared to $15k for the 
middle-buffer group and $95k for the high buffer group. 
Low buffer households face median minimum mortgage 
repayments of around $2,600 a month, compared with $2,400 
for the middle buffer group and $2,200 for the highest-buffer 
group. 
Low buffer households also spend more on fuel, at around 
$170 a month compared with $160 for the middle buffer 
group and $150 for the high buffer group.

We can use average pump prices to estimate what this 
expenditure implies for fuel volumes. Low-buffer households 
consume around 70 litres a month, middle-buffer households 
67 litres a month and the high-buffer group around 62 litres. 
This compares to an average of 81, 80 and 70 respectively 
through 2024-25. 
Median fuel spend by customer

### Page 5

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts are based are reasonable, 
the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
Yet despite this greater exposure, the gap in broader spending 
behaviour has narrowed. High-buffer households continue 
to spend more on discretionary categories, averaging around 
$1,940 a month compared with $1,740 for low-buffer 
households. However, their discretionary share has been 
broadly stable since 2023, while the shares of low- and 
middle-buffer households have risen by close to a percentage 
point bringing them more in line with the high-buffer group.

Some of the convergence reflects a longer run down-trend in 
fuel spending that has been more pronounced amongst low 
and middle buffer households. This lowering in fuel spend lifts 
the share available for other categories.

Essential spending excluding fuel, meanwhile, has changed 
little across the three cohorts since 2023. The shift in 
spending patterns has largely occurred on the discretionary 
side.

The result is a somewhat mixed picture. Low-buffer 
households remain considerably more exposed to mortgage 
and fuel costs and have far smaller savings cushions. But their 
discretionary spending has nevertheless moved closer to that 
of better-buffered households, suggesting some recovery in 
spending flexibility despite these constraints. 
Conclusion
Aggregate household finances remain resilient: spending and 
savings both increased in Q3 and mortgage buffers continued 
to rebuild. Discretionary spending has also held up despite the 
sharp increase in fuel costs.

But aggregate resilience masks a much more uneven 
distribution of pressure. Low-buffer households 
simultaneously face higher mortgage repayments, greater fuel 
exposure and much smaller savings cushions. While these 
households have so far maintained discretionary spending, 
their capacity to absorb further pressure is clearly more 
limited.
About the Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel
Developed by DataX, Westpac’s data analytics service for 
institutional and business banking customers, the dataset links 
transaction activity with balance sheet information to give 
a complete view of income, spending, saving and borrowing 
flows. 
The resulting sample of over one million customers provides 
an accurate representation of trends across the wider 
Australian consumer. Monthly observations are available back 
to January 2019 with timely updates provided a few weeks 
after the end of each month. As such, it is perfectly suited to 
quickly tracing the impact and responses to events like interest 
rate moves and energy price shocks.

F I R S T  T O  M A R K E T

CTCP VÀNG BẠC ĐÁ QUÝ PHÚ NHUẬN (PNJ)  
Cập nhật ĐHĐCĐ bất thường 21/10/2026

Tái cơ cấu để xử lý áp lực thanh khoản  
— ĐHĐCĐ bất thường PNJ ngày 21/10/2026 đã thông qua điều chỉnh kế hoạch kinh 
doanh và phương án phát hành riêng lẻ để bổ sung vốn tái cấu trúc. Ban lãnh đạo đặt 
ra các vấn đề trọng yếu là đáp ứng nhu cầu thanh khoản và chuẩn bị nguồn lực để thực 
hiện các cam kết thu đổi đã tích lũy trong quá khứ. Bên cạnh đó, các cổ đông tham dự 
cũng đặt các câu hỏi liên quan đến tình hình hoạt động qua các điểm chính (1) dòng 
tiền hoạt động kinh doanh; (2) diễn biến thị trường sau khi PNJ áp dụng chính sách thu 
đổi mới; (3) hiệu quả hoạt động sau tái cấu trúc.

Nội dung chính tại đại hội 
— Theo Ban lãnh đạo, thông tin liên quan đến P-Lab từ đầu tháng 7 khiến khách hàng gia 
tăng bán lại sản phẩm. Tổng giá trị thu đổi và mua lại trong tháng đạt gần 6,000 tỷ VND, 
so với khoảng 7,000 tỷ VND trong cả năm 2025. Theo BCTC riêng, nguồn tiền khả dụng 
giảm từ khoảng 3,700 tỷ VND cuối Q2/2026 xuống 770 tỷ VND cuối Q3/2026, đã bao 
gồm khoản vay 700 tỷ VND từ Chủ tịch HĐQT. Tỷ trọng kim cương và trang sức kim 
cương trong tổng tồn kho tăng từ 41% lên 52%. 
— Q3/2026 ghi nhận lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND trước phần tăng trích lập dự phòng. Để giảm 
áp lực thanh toán, PNJ kéo dài thời gian chi trả đối với kim cương lên 120 ngày từ tháng 
07/2026. Thay đổi chính sách cũng ảnh hưởng đến niềm tin khách hàng và sức mua, cụ 
thể doanh thu và lợi nhuận gộp Q3/2026 đạt lần lượt 6,995/609 tỷ VND, giảm 14%/62% 
YoY. Chi phí bán hàng và quản lý giảm 13% YoY chưa đủ bù đắp mức giảm lợi nhuận 
gộp, khiến PNJ lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND, so với lãi 496 tỷ VND trong Q3/2025.

F I R S T  T O  M A R K E T 
 — PNJ nâng giả định tỷ lệ thu đổi trên giá trị thu đổi còn lại lên 80%, so với 28.8% đối với 
nhóm sản phẩm giai đoạn 2020–2026 trong ước tính tại BCTC bán niên 2026. Tổng dự 
phòng tổn thất dự kiến tăng từ 2,267 tỷ VND lên 7,071 tỷ VND. Kế hoạch điều chỉnh ghi 
nhận doanh thu 39,057 tỷ VND, lỗ trước thuế 6,059 tỷ VND và lỗ sau thuế 6,271 tỷ VND. 
Theo Ban lãnh đạo, LNST loại trừ dự phòng ước khoảng 800 tỷ VND, chủ yếu nhờ kết 
quả Q1/2026. Khoản dự phòng phản ánh tổn thất ước tính cho toàn bộ kịch bản, không 
đồng nghĩa toàn bộ giá trị sẽ được chi trả ngay trong kỳ. 
— Giá trị cam kết thu đổi tích lũy đối với kim cương và trang sức kim cương tại cuối 
Q3/2026 được PNJ ước tính khoảng 21,000 tỷ VND. Với hàng đã bán, doanh nghiệp dự 
kiến chuyển đổi quyền lợi khách hàng sang sản phẩm và các chương trình ưu đãi. Với 
sản phẩm bán mới, chính sách thu đổi định hướng theo giá thị trường tại thời điểm 
giao dịch, thay cho tỷ lệ cố định trên giá trị hóa đơn, nhằm hạn chế tiếp tục tích lũy 
nghĩa vụ tiềm tàng trong tương lai theo cơ chế cũ.

Phương án bổ sung vốn và tái cơ cấu hoạt động kinh doanh 
— Đại hội thông qua phương án chào bán tối đa 550 triệu cổ phiếu. Nguồn vốn dự kiến 
phân bổ 60% cho kế hoạch kinh doanh gắn với điều chỉnh chính sách thu đổi, 20% bổ 
sung vốn lưu động và phần còn lại bổ sung vốn lưu động và/hoặc trả vay ngắn hạn. 
Nhu cầu vốn Q4/2026 và Q1/2027 lần lượt khoảng 3,100 tỷ VND và 2,800 tỷ VND. Giá và 
khối lượng phát hành thực tế chưa được xác định tại đại hội.  
— Doanh nghiệp dự kiến đóng 25–30 cửa hàng và mở khoảng 10 cửa hàng (có chọn lọc) 
trong năm 2026, tương ứng giảm ròng 15–20 cửa hàng; đồng thời đặt mục tiêu giảm 
20–30% chi phí nhân sự. Cổ tức tiền mặt năm 2026 được điều chỉnh về 0%; cổ tức 2025 
về 10% mệnh giá và đã chi trả. 
— 100% mẫu kim cương được AIGS và Vinacontrol kiểm định độc lập là kim cương thiên 
nhiên, phù hợp thông số 4C. PNJ dự kiến trang bị máy kiểm định tại các cửa hàng trọng 
điểm để khách hàng kiểm tra trực tiếp.

Đánh giá của KBSV 
— KBSV cho rằng huy động vốn từ phát hành riêng lẻ là phương án phù hợp ở thời điểm 
hiện tại trong bối cảnh lãi suất tăng cao, giúp PNJ cân đối thanh khoản và duy trì hoạt 
động liên tục. Tuy nhiên, với khối lượng phát hành lớn và chưa có phương án chi tiết, 
doanh nghiệp sẽ cần thêm thời gian để hoàn thành phương án phát hành và cân đối 
giữa lợi ích của cổ đông và doanh nghiệp.  
— Nhu cầu yếu tại Trung Quốc và cạnh tranh từ kim cương nuôi cấy giá thấp tiếp tục gây 
áp lực lên giá kim cương tự nhiên, khiến PNJ gặp khó khăn trong việc tiêu thụ tồn kho 
nhóm kim cương  và gia tăng tổn thất khi mua lại theo chính sách cũ. Thực tế, tỷ trọng 
kim cương và trang sức kim cương trong tổng tồn kho tăng từ 41% cuối Q2/2026 lên 
52% cuối Q3/2026, với giá trị ước khoảng 9,000 tỷ VND. KBSV cho rằng cần theo dõi (1) 
nhu cầu thị trường trong giai đoạn cuối năm; (2) tốc độ giảm tồn kho và dòng tiền thu 
về từ bán hàng; và (3) giá trị khách hàng bán lại. Diễn biến các yếu tố này sẽ quyết định 
khả năng thu hồi vốn, mức độ phục hồi biên lợi nhuận và nhu cầu trích lập bổ sung 
trong quá trình xử lý. 
— Ước tính bán niên sử dụng tỷ lệ tổn thất 20.3% đối với hàng bán trong giai đoạn 2020–
2026 dự kiến được thu mua lại, thấp hơn mức 28.9% trên các giao dịch mua lại thực tế 
từ 01/07–03/09/2026. Vì vậy, bên cạnh giả định thu đổi 80%, mức tổn thất khi xử lý 
hàng mua lại là yếu tố quan trọng cần theo dõi. Việc chuyển hạch toán dự phòng từ 
SG&A sang giá vốn tại kỳ soát xét bán niên cũng cho thấy áp lực biên lợi nhuận bị co 
hẹp trong quá trình tái cấu trúc về mặt kế toán.

Document 314: EcoCharts _ Business creations and bankruptcies - Partial update with monthly data as of September 2026 (2026-11-13)

Source PDF: all report pdf/EcoCharts _ Business creations and bankruptcies - Partial update with monthly data as of September 2026 (2026-11-13).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas

### Page 1

13/11/2026 1
November 2026
BUSINESS CREATIONS AND BANKRUPTCIES IN FRANCE: DOUBLE-DIGIT GROWTH IN BUSINESS CREATIONS,
LOWER INCREASE IN BANKRUPTCIES, AND STABLE NON-PERFORMING LOANS – PARTIAL UPDATE WITH
MONTHLY DATA AS OF SEPTEMBER 2026
ECO CHARTS | BUSINESS CREATIONS AND BANKRUPTCIES IS A NEW PUBLICATION AIMED AT PROVIDING AN OVERVIEW OF THE DYNAMICS
OF BUSINESS CREATIONS AND BANKRUPTCIES IN FRANCE, AS WELL AS THERISKS ASSOCIATED WITH THE LATTER. WE WILL PUBLISH

REGULAR UPDATES TO THIS PUBLICATION.

### Page 2

Double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans
2ECOCHARTSECONOMIC STUDIES
• Business creations rose by 16% y/y as of Q3 2026, marking an acceleration compared with the growth seen in 2025 (5.3% y/y).
• This increase was driven by sectors associated with new information and communication technologies, where the trend is gaining
momentum.
• Meanwhile, business bankruptcies rose by 1.7% y/y as of Q3 2026, according to the data from the Banque de France (vs. 3.6% y/y in

2025). After a more pronounced rise in the first quarter (8% y/y), this increase has since slowed. This moderation could continue, as,
according to Altares, payment delays have decreased to an average of 12.3 days in the second quarter of 2026 (14.1 days a year
earlier), compared with the European average of 13.1 days.
• The rise in business bankruptcies is most pronounced in the services sector, which has simultaneously seen an increase in business

creations. This trend is clear in the retail sector, with a 26% y/y increase in business creations as of Q3 2026, while business
bankruptcies are up 3% y/y.
• In construction and manufacturing, business bankruptcies have been declining since Q2 2026.
• The level of the average ratio of non-performing loans is moderately high and has remained stable for the past year (4.0% of total
loans and advances in Q2 2026). More cyclical than in other sectors, the ratio of non-performing loans in construction reached its

highest level since 2019, at 9.5%.
• The share of underperforming loans, but not yet non-performing (in Stage 2, according to IFRS 9 terminology), which provides an
indication of the future trend in non-performing loans, is stable, standing at 15.4% q/q, and remains below its all-time high (16.1%).
• Despite a significant rise since 2021, the economic weight of bankruptcies — which allows them to be weighted by a proxy for
company size — remains in September, on average, below its Q1 2025 peak (0.54 compared with 0.61, respectively). The economic

weight of bankrupted small businesses decreased but still stands at a high level (2.03).
For more information, please refer to our publication: Chart of the week | French corporates: peaks in creations put peaks in bankruptcies into
perspective. July 22, 2026 – Economic Research – BNP Paribas

Companies subject to collective proceedings (receivership and judicial liquidation). Monthly data from the
Banque de France aggregated over a quarter to minimise the risk of calendar effects
Business creations Insee Monthly
Survey of business creations (all types, companies, sole traders, micro-entrepreneurs). Monthly data from
INSEE aggregated over a quarter to minimise the risk of calendar effects

Non-performing loans ratio EBA Quarterly
Gross non performing loans and advances at amortised cost / Total gross loans and advances at
amortised cost
Share of underperforming loans (Stage 2 IFRS 9) ECB Quarterly
Loans and advances in Stage 2 / Total loans and advances. Loans and advances at amortised cost and at
fair value through equity
Economic weight of bankruptcies Banque de France Monthly
Total outstanding bank loans to newly bankrupt NFCs over a one-year period, calculated as the figure for

the month in which the firm is declared bankrupt, relative to the average outstanding bank loans to both
healthy and bankrupt NFCs during the same period.
Share of 90+ days past due payments Urssaf Monthly Contributions still to be collected by Urssaf / Amount of contributions due to Urssaf

We have published our 2027 Macro Outlook: Steady Baseline, Downside Risks. We flesh 
out the Global Market implications and key asset views here, highlighting 10 
investment themes that are likely to dominate price action in 2027. More detailed 
outlooks for the individual asset classes are forthcoming.  
A maturing market cycle: Stable global growth and declining inflation make for 1.
a friendly macro baseline, but risks are to the downside as the market cycle ages. 
The AI investment impulse peaks: AI has contributed more to market gains 2.
than economic growth, so a bigger test for markets than the economy.  
Passing—or dropping—the baton: Equity gains move from AI infrastructure to 3.
other areas, but we see risks if tighter financial conditions thwart the transition 
or markets look further ahead. 
Energy—balancing risk and risk premium: Our baseline is for a gradual decline 4.
in energy prices, but an escalation-driven spike remains one of the largest 
near-term risks to markets. 
Bonds reclaim macro and hedging value: Higher running yield, more paths to 5.
relief, and cheap valuations argue for higher bond allocations, as supply shocks 
give way to demand stresses. 
A more cyclical Dollar, Asia’s revaluing currencies: Lower inflation and less 6.
tech exceptionalism should make for a softer Dollar through 2027, especially 
versus Asia’s revaluing currencies (JPY, CNY). 
Europe & UK—global shocks and local shifts: European markets are under 7.
pressure from global shocks and local concerns, which may persist until the 
French election, keeping EUR downside attractive. 
The stress of financing the boom: Record credit and equity issuance has been 8.
digestible so far, but with discounted pricing needed in credit, and further 
widening in US credit spreads is likely.

Goldman Sachs Global Markets Outlook 2027

### Page 3

Markets Outlook 2027: Growing Pains

1. A maturing market cycle 
Stable growth, declining inflation make for a friendly macro backdrop. n
But risks are to the downside, and an ageing market cycle is facing more headwinds. n
Higher rates, wider spreads, and a peaking AI impulse mean the broad asset picture n
is more two-sided ... 
... and argues for more diversified allocations, including bonds and hedges. n
Our headline macro forecasts for 2027 are quite friendly: stable growth, declining 
inflation and peaking policy rates. But as the economic cycle extends, the market cycle is 
increasingly showing its age. For 2026, our view was that even though asset market 
valuations were running ahead of the macro, a positive cyclical backdrop including 
strong investment growth should dominate. While the Iran war has upended many 
forecasts, even without the energy shock the market is running into more conventional 
late-cycle headwinds. Real rates are higher, credit spreads are wider and volatility is 
picking up, especially under the surface. On top of this, the AI impulse, which has been a 
key support to markets through the shocks this year, looks poised to peak in the coming 
year. 
That is likely to prove a bigger headwind to the profit and asset market picture than 
growth, given its larger role in supporting equity markets. Markets have already moved 
some way towards reflecting that the extraordinary surge in profitability in the AI 
infrastructure complex is unlikely to be extrapolated. So a flattening in the gains there 
may be offset by gains further down the waterfall among AI users or other cyclical parts 
of the market, especially if energy prices and rates fall more. But that is a tricky 
balancing act, and further boosts to equity prices are likely to come alongside a higher 
base level of volatility. There are also risks that AI-related drawdowns tighten financial 
conditions more meaningfully, or that energy and policy relief do not arrive on time. 
That leaves the broader risk-asset picture looking more two-sided than it has for several 
years, arguing for more diversified and better-hedged portfolios. 
12 November 2026   3
Goldman Sachs Global Markets Outlook 2027

### Page 4

2. The AI investment impulse peaks 
The AI investment impulse peak is a bigger test for markets than the economy. n
That impulse has been a larger contributor to growth in market value and earnings n
than in GDP. 
Markets have already de-rated AI infrastructure beneficiaries. n
Valuation excesses are less visible, so the focus is on sustainability of record profits. n
Coming into 2026, we expected continued acceleration in AI investment to dominate 
valuation concerns in equities, but likely amid higher volatility and wider credit spreads.  
Although we expect solid AI investment growth to continue, our forecasts imply that the 
investment growth impulse—additional AI investment as a share of GDP—will peak in 
early 2027. From a macro perspective, this should be a manageable event. But it may be 
a bigger test for markets for three reasons. First, the AI impulse has been a much larger 
contributor to growth in earnings and market value than to growth in GDP. Second, 
AI-related equities have taken credit for significant macro productivity gains, especially 
companies benefiting directly from the infrastructure boom, that have made them more 
vulnerable to any signs that we are on a less optimistic path. Third, the point at which 
growth rates peak (a “second derivative” turning point) often matters, even if underlying 
growth rates (the “first derivative”) remain healthy. Moving through that peak allows 
markets to place limits on the upside of an uncertain distribution and, as growth 
decelerates, the period of positive surprises to expectations often ends too, as we may 
be starting to see for the AI capex cycle. 
Markets have already been reflecting these concerns. Since June, we have seen a 
significant de-rating of companies levered to the AI infrastructure theme, which are 
most sensitive to shifts in the investment outlook and had been the biggest market 
beneficiaries in the first half of 2026. Moreover, robust profits, and a rising share of

### Page 5

income going to listed companies, have helped keep both the imbalances and the deep 
valuation excesses that we saw in the late 1990s at bay. The key question in this cycle is 
whether the market is correctly assessing how much of the recent acceleration in profits, 
which has taken S&P 500 earnings to a record high relative to GDP, will prove persistent, 
especially as the investment cycle itself fades. Without the capital inflows from the EM 
crises in 1997 and 1998—or the rate cuts that goods price deflation allowed during that 
tech cycle—increased financing needs have also been putting clearer upward pressure 
on the cost of capital.

3. Passing—or dropping—the baton 
Strong levels of AI investment growth and profitability should limit the pressure. n
And the baton may be passed from AI infrastructure to other market areas. n
But we see risks of a larger aggregate shock if the market looks forward, or other n
areas cannot take up the slack. 
Tighter financial conditions or credit conditions could create a feedback loop. n
Two things give us comfort in terms of how bumpy this transition through the peak of 
the investment cycle will prove to be. The first is that although the investment impulse 
from AI looks poised to peak, growth in both AI investment and earnings should remain 
firmly positive in 2027, especially earlier in the year. We expect S&P 500 earnings growth 
to decelerate from 36% in 2026 to a still-robust 11% in 2027. This may ultimately help to 
limit the de-rating of even the most directly affected areas and should reduce the 
impact on broader growth-sensitive assets, though it should also imply less relief in core 
rates pricing. The second is that the baton may be passed, at least to a degree, from the 
direct beneficiaries of the AI investment boom to other areas of the market. This 
includes other areas of the AI complex, such as the hyperscalers, if monetization paths 
become more visible; companies that may begin to show benefits from AI productivity 
enhancements; or non-AI companies that see some relief as the crowding out of capital

### Page 6

available to them eases. 
The risk is that what could be more of a rotation—and a flattening in the upward trend in 
asset markets—becomes a bigger aggregate shock. This could happen because the 
market begins to look further forward to the peak in the investment share itself (in 2028 
on our forecasts); because the passing of the baton to other sectors does not take place 
as quickly or as smoothly as needed; or because feedback loops from financial and 
credit conditions accelerate more quickly than in our baseline. We think the risk to 
markets is higher than the risk to the economic picture on each front, because the boom 
itself has had its largest impact there. For that reason, the most likely channel through 
which AI worries could lead to broader economic downside is through financial 
conditions themselves.

4. Energy—balancing risk and risk premium 
An escalation-driven oil spike remains one of the largest near-term risks to markets. n
Our baseline is for a gradual decline in energy prices, but the hedge value of energy n
is still high. 
Downside tail to energy prices with clearer/faster resolution. n
Risk premium has fluctuated, so hedge value also varies over time. n
A potential oil price spike driven by another escalation in the war with Iran remains one 
of the biggest near-term risks to the markets and growth picture. Particularly after the 
fall in oil prices so far in November, pricing is vulnerable to any broadening in the conflict 
and new supply disruptions. We would expect that to hurt equities, support the Dollar 
against European currencies and put upward pressure on rates, all else equal. Europe, 
too, faces a precarious winter, given low storage levels in natural gas which leave the 
region vulnerable to risks from both supply and weather. If those risks are avoided, our 
baseline is for a gradual decline in energy prices over the coming year even without full 
resolution of the conflict. Despite the ongoing war with Iran, oil market supply has 
largely been restored (even if refining capacity is limited), but risks of escalation and 
further disruptions have generally led the market to demand a fluctuating but 
meaningful risk premium above where we think the fundamental supply-demand picture 
would leave them.

### Page 7

Our baseline is a gradual recovery in supply and reduction in energy prices, leaving 
energy and core commodity returns more muted. But the risk of more serious 
disruptions is real, and energy prices could move significantly higher in those scenarios 
at a time when equities and, potentially, bonds could both be under pressure. The 
hedging value of the asset class remains high for that reason. The wrinkle is that the risk 
premium in oil has varied substantially and conditions the value of the hedge. When the 
risk premium falls, as it has done more recently, that hedging value is likely to be higher. 
When that risk premium is very high, as it was at the end of October, oil markets are 
more vulnerable to downside tails if news flow is better than expected, and outright 
longs (rather than options) have generally been more risky. The downside tail from faster 
progress towards resolution than we expect (not just in Iran, but in Ukraine too) is one of 
the clearest sources of upside risks for broader markets. In that scenario, energy prices 
could come down more quickly, supporting both bonds and equities.

5. Bonds reclaim macro and hedging value 
Rates moved up in 2026, on oil, hikes, AI spending and fiscal deficits. n
Central bank pricing now too hawkish relative to our baseline. n
Bonds should be a better hedge as supply shocks give way to demand stresses. n
Higher running yield, more paths to relief, and cheap valuations argue for higher n
bond allocations. 
Interest rates moved higher in 2026 after a low point on the eve of the Iran war. The 
resultant surge in energy prices and central bank pricing has been the primary catalyst 
across most DM and EM rates markets, but real rates, especially in the US, have 
continued to move higher as investment demand from the AI build-out interacts with 
sustained fiscal deficits. Despite that backdrop, we think that central bank pricing in 
many economies, including the US and the UK, is too hawkish relative to what is likely to 
be delivered. Even with the energy shock, the underlying inflation story is still fairly 
benign, which means that policy rates are likely to peak at lower levels relative to 
2023-2024 and reverse earlier. The risk to this view is that the energy shock extends 
further or inflation remains stickier and the labour market proves more resilient, as it did 
this year. 
Bonds have been a poor hedge in this environment of energy supply shocks, rate hikes

### Page 8

and real rate pressures from private and public sector spending. But we think the case 
for duration has shifted in a more constructive direction for several reasons. First, on a 
static basis, bond yields are now providing a good running yield and a decent spread to 
cash. Second, valuations are cheap (we see UST 10y yields at 4.6% by end-2027), 
positioning is underweight, and there are paths to some shallow rate relief from current 
levels under our benign baseline scenario, and scope for larger moves if AI spending 
decelerates more quickly. Third, the increase in yields has started to create stresses that 
indicate that a higher level of real rates may be difficult to sustain. Bonds are already 
starting to illustrate their value as a hedge against shocks of these types—with Bunds 
rallying as French sovereign spreads widened and USTs rallying as tech stocks came 
under pressure. Although energy spikes remain a near-term risk, we are more positive 
about bond allocations even after the recent rally, in part because of that hedging value.

6. A more cyclical Dollar, Asia’s revaluing currencies 
After tight ranges in 2026, lower inflation, energy prices make for a softer Dollar. n
But a bigger USD swing could come if US tech exceptionalism erodes. n
JPY is a more attractive hedge with ‘team Japan’ focused on limiting Yen weakness. n
CNY appreciation has further to run given undervaluation and a surging trade n
surplus.  
The broad Dollar is finishing 2026 on the strong side of tight ranges, buoyed by a sturdy 
US nominal growth backdrop, higher energy prices, and a shift to more hawkish Fed 
pricing. Under our baseline forecast, all three impulses should fade: alongside trend 
growth we expect inflation in the US to converge towards target, energy prices should 
decline as the Middle East disruptions gradually normalize and we expect the Fed to 
move from hikes to cuts as we head into late 2027 and early 2028. That should make for

### Page 9

a softer Dollar backdrop, and we have pencilled in a roughly 3% fall in the broad 
trade-weighted Dollar in 2027. 
But the real swing factor for FX is likely to be how the peak in AI-related tech spending 
affects US equity returns. Over the past several years, tech-related macro and market 
outperformance in the US has sucked in equity capital flows from the rest of the world 
and has been the primary factor supporting the Dollar’s premium valuation. Our 
baseline forecasts envisage a more even distribution of returns globally rather than US 
underperformance. But if US tech performance erodes meaningfully, that would likely 
come alongside some cyclical underperformance of the Dollar rather than a traditional 
‘safe haven’ bid, as we have already seen in short periods over the past couple of years. 
Instead, we would expect the undervalued Yen to prove a better haven in that scenario, 
given its typical sensitivity to equities and rates falling together. With Japanese policy 
authorities also now focused on limiting further Yen depreciation and encouraging 
inflows into Japanese assets, we think that the Yen should increasingly be a more 
attractive hedge to pro-cyclical portfolios. We also see scope for the Yuan to continue to 
appreciate gradually as China’s trade surplus keeps growing, and forward markets 
underestimate how far this process can run given the deep undervaluation starting 
point. While domestic demand is also very weak, we have argued that China needs both 
currency appreciation and expansionary policy, two instruments to hit two targets, to 
move towards better external and internal balance.

7. Europe & UK—global shocks and local shifts 
European markets have been under pressure from energy ToT, higher global yields n
and sovereign stresses. 
Even if global shocks abate, local concerns still persist until the French election, n
keeping EUR downside attractive.
